,
\lambda=\frac{1}{5}
.

Then, for the vectorial composite operator
{V}_{\mu}(x)
, we first observe that

\displaystyle V_{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\varphi^{\dagger}(x)(D_{\mu}\varphi)(x)

(4.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\varphi^{\dagger}(x)A_{\mu}(x)\varphi(x)-\frac{1}{2}i\varphi^{\dagger}(x)\partial_{\mu}\varphi(x)+\frac{1}{2}i\varphi(x)\partial_{\mu}\varphi^{\dagger}(x)-i\partial_{\mu}O(x),

and since we know that the last term is gauge-invariant, the first three terms together are also gauge-invariant. We thus define a new gauge-invariant operator

\displaystyle V^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e\varphi^{\dagger}(x)A_{\mu}(x)\varphi(x)-\frac{1}{2}i\varphi^{\dagger}(x)\partial_{\mu}\varphi(x)+\frac{1}{2}i\varphi(x)\partial_{\mu}\varphi^{\dagger}(x),

(4.26)

expanding the scalar field
\varphi(x)
we find

\displaystyle V^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}e(v+h(x))^{2}A_{\mu}(x)+\frac{1}{2}e\rho^{2}(x)A_{\mu}(x)+(v+h(x))\partial_{\mu}\rho(x)-\rho(x)\partial_{\mu}h(x)

(4.27)

so that

\displaystyle\braket{{V^{\prime}}_{\mu}(x){V^{\prime}}_{v}(y)}

\displaystyle\overset{\varphi\rightarrow\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(v+h+i\rho)}{=}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\Bigg\{-e^{2}v^{4}\langle A_{\mu}(x)A_{\nu}(y)\rangle-4e^{2}v^{3}\langle h(x)A_{\mu}(x)A_{\nu}(y)\rangle

(4.28)

\displaystyle-2e^{2}v^{2}\langle h(x)^{2}A_{\mu}(x)A_{\nu}(y)\rangle-4e^{2}v^{2}\langle h(x)A_{\mu}(x)h(y)A_{\nu}(y)\rangle

\displaystyle-2e^{2}v^{2}\langle\rho(x)^{2}A_{\mu}(x)A_{\nu}(y)\rangle-2ev^{2}\partial^{x}{}_{\mu}\langle h(x)\rho(x)A_{v}(y)\rangle

\displaystyle+4ev^{2}\langle\partial^{x}{}_{\mu}h(x)\rho(x)A_{\nu}(y)\rangle-2ev^{3}\partial^{x}{}_{\mu}\langle\rho(x)A_{\nu}(y)\rangle

\displaystyle-4ev^{2}\partial^{x}{}_{\mu}\langle\rho(x)h(y)A_{v}(y)\rangle-2v\partial^{x}{}_{\mu}\partial^{y}{}_{\nu}\langle h(x)\rho(x)\rho(y)\rangle

\displaystyle+4v\partial^{y}{}_{\nu}\langle\partial^{x}{}_{\mu}h(x)\rho(x)\rho(y)\rangle-\partial^{x}{}_{\mu}\partial^{y}{}_{\nu}\langle h(x)\rho(x)h(y)\rho(y)\rangle

\displaystyle+4\langle\partial^{x}{}_{\mu}h(x)\rho(x)h(y)\partial^{y}{}_{\nu}\rho(y)\rangle-v^{2}\partial^{x}{}_{\mu}\partial^{y}{}_{\nu}\langle\rho(x)\rho(y)\rangle\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+O(\hbar^{2}),

where we have discarded the terms that do not have one-loop contributions.
In momentum space, we can split the two-point function into transverse and longitudinal parts in the usual way:

\displaystyle\langle{V^{\prime}}_{\mu}(p){V^{\prime}}_{\nu}(-p)\rangle=\langle{V^{\prime}}(p){V^{\prime}}(-p)\rangle^{T}\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}+\langle V^{\prime}(p)V^{\prime}(-p)\rangle^{L}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}\,,

(4.29)

where we have introduced the transverse and longitudinal projectors, given respectively by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu\nu}-\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{2}}\,.

(4.30)

At tree-level, we find in momentum space

\displaystyle\langle V^{\prime}_{\mu}(p)V^{\prime}_{\nu}(-p)\rangle_{\text{\text{tree}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\left(-e^{2}v^{4}\langle A_{\mu}(p)A_{\nu}(-p)\rangle-v^{2}p_{\mu}p_{\nu}\langle\rho(p)\rho(-p)\rangle\right)

(4.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left(e^{2}v^{4}\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}+e^{2}v^{4}\frac{\xi}{p^{2}+\xi m^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}+v^{2}\frac{p^{2}}{p^{2}+\xi m^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{2}v^{4}}{4}\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}+v^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}.

We can now analyze the connected diagrams for each term, up to one-loop order, through the action (
3.4
). We calculated the one-loop diagrams in Appendix
B.7
. Let us start with the transverse part. Looking at the diagrams in Figure
B.2
, we can see that the one-loop correlation function will have the following structure

\displaystyle\braket{V^{\prime}(p)V^{\prime}(-p)}^{T,1-loop}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{A^{V}_{fin}(p^{2})+\delta A^{V}_{div}(p^{2})}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}+\frac{B^{V}_{fin}(p^{2})+\delta B^{V}_{div}(p^{2})}{(p^{2}+m^{2})}

(4.32)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C^{V}_{fin}(p^{2})+\delta C^{V}_{div}(p^{2})

where
(A^{V}_{fin},B^{V}_{fin},C^{V}_{fin})
stand for the finite parts and
(\delta A^{V}_{div},\delta B^{V}_{div},\delta C^{V}_{div})
for the purely divergent terms, i.e. the pole terms in
\frac{1}{\epsilon}
obtained by means of the dimensional regularization, namely

\displaystyle\delta A^{V}_{div}

\displaystyle\overset{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{e^{4}v^{4}}{2(4\pi)^{2}\epsilon}\Big(\frac{1}{3}p^{2}-6(\frac{e^{2}}{\lambda}-\frac{1}{2})e^{2}v^{2}+3\lambda v^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\delta B^{V}_{div}

\displaystyle\overset{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}}{(4\pi)^{2}\epsilon}(6\frac{e^{6}v^{2}}{\lambda}-3e^{4}v^{2}-\frac{e^{2}p^{2}}{3}+3e^{2}\lambda v^{2})

\displaystyle\delta C^{V}_{div}

\displaystyle\overset{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6(4\pi)^{2}\epsilon}(9e^{2}v^{2}-p^{2}-3\lambda v^{2})

(4.33)

and

\displaystyle A^{V}_{fin}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{4}v^{4}}{2(4\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\,\Bigg\{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}x

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m_{h}^{2}(1-x)(1-\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}x+m_{h}^{2}(1-x)}{\mu^{2}})+m_{h}^{2}(1-\ln\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{m^{4}}{m_{h}^{2}}(1-3\ln\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}})+2m^{2}\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}x+m_{h}^{2}(1-x)}{\mu^{2}}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle B^{V}_{fin}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}}{18m_{h}^{2}p^{2}(4\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{3m_{h}^{4}\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}-7p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{2}\left(2p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}-5m^{2}\right)+\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}\right){}^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{xm_{h}^{2}+m^{2}(1-x)+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3\left(m_{h}^{3}-m^{2}m_{h}\right){}^{2}+9p^{2}\left(m^{2}m_{h}^{2}+3m_{h}^{4}+2m^{4}\right)+2p^{4}m_{h}^{2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}\left(p^{2}-m^{2}\right)+m_{h}^{4}+18m^{2}p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle C^{V}_{fin}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{36(4\pi)^{2}p^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{3m^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

(4.34)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{2}\left(-m_{h}^{2}+m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+6m_{h}^{2}\left(p^{2}-m^{2}\right)-5p^{2}\left(3m_{h}^{2}-9m^{2}+p^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3\left(2m_{h}^{2}\left(p^{2}-m^{2}\right)+m_{h}^{4}+m^{4}-10m^{2}p^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+xm^{2}+(1-x)m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{4}+3m^{4}-54m^{2}p^{2}+3p^{4}\Bigg\}.

The divergent terms
(\delta A^{V}_{div},\delta B^{V}_{div},\delta C^{V}_{div})
can be eliminated by means of the Lagrangian counterterms as well as by suitable counterterms in the external part of the action
S^{V}_{J}
accounting for the introduction of the composite operator
V^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)
, i.e.

\displaystyle S^{V}_{J}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S+\int d^{4}x\Bigg[(1+\delta Z_{div}^{V,0})J_{\mu}(x)V_{\mu}(x)+(1+\delta Z^{V}_{div})\frac{J_{\mu}(x)J_{\mu}(x)}{2}\Bigg],

(4.35)

where
J_{\mu}(x)
is a BRST invariant dimension one source needed to define the generator
Z^{c}(J)
of the connected Green function
\braket{V^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)V^{\prime}_{v}(y)}
:

\displaystyle\braket{V^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)V^{\prime}_{v}(y)}=\frac{\delta^{2}Z^{c}(J)}{\delta J_{\mu}(x)\delta J_{v}(y)}|_{J=0},

(4.36)

and like in the scalar case, we have the freedom of introducing a pure contact BRST invariant term in the external source
J_{\mu}(x)
:

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\,\frac{1}{2}(\beta v^{2}\,J_{\mu}(x)J_{\mu}(x)+\gamma J_{\mu}(x)\partial^{2}J_{\mu}(x)+\sigma(\partial_{\mu}J_{\mu}(x))^{2}),

(4.37)

which can be arbitrarily added to the action (
4.35
). Including such a term in (
4.35
) will have the effect of adding a dimensionless constant to
G^{T}_{VV}(p^{2})=\braket{V^{\prime}(p)V^{\prime}(-p)^{T}}
, i.e.

\displaystyle G^{T}_{VV}(p^{2})\rightarrow G^{T}_{VV}(p^{2})+\beta v^{2}+\gamma p^{2},

(4.38)

where we notice that the last term in (
4.37
) does not contribute to the transversal part of the propagator.
In particular,
\beta
and
\gamma
can be choosen so that (
4.38
) becomes

\displaystyle G^{T}_{VV}(p^{2})-G^{T}_{VV}(0)-p^{2}\frac{\partial G^{T}_{VV}(p^{2})}{\partial p^{2}}|_{p=0},

(4.39)

see Figure
4.5
. It is a Green’s function that obeys a two substracted KL representation, see section
C.3
.
Following the steps in the same way as for the scalar composite field, (
4.11
)-(
5.52
), we find

\displaystyle\langle V^{\prime}(p)V^{\prime}(-p)\rangle^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{2}v^{4}}{4}\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}}+\frac{\hbar e^{2}v^{4}}{4}\frac{\Pi^{T}_{VV}(p^{2})}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2}),

(4.40)

with

\displaystyle\Pi^{T}_{VV}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{9(4\pi)^{2}e^{2}v^{4}m_{h}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{-18m^{4}(m_{h}^{4}+m^{4})+9m_{h}^{2}p^{4}(m_{h}^{2}+m^{2})

(4.41)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{2}p^{2}\big[2p^{2}(m_{h}^{2}-5m^{2})+(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2})^{2}+p^{4}\big]\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}(1-x)+m^{2}x+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)+2m_{h}^{2}p^{6}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\big[p^{2}(m_{h}^{2}+11m^{2})+6m^{4}-p^{4}\big]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3m^{2}\big[-m_{h}^{2}p^{4}+p^{2}(-m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{2}m^{2}+36m^{4})+18m^{6}\big]\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3p^{2}(m_{h}^{6}+10m_{h}^{4}m^{2}+m_{h}^{2}m^{4}+12m^{6})\Bigg\},

and following the steps (
3.51
)-(
3.53
), we find

\displaystyle G_{VV}^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{2}v^{4}}{4}\Big(\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}-\Pi^{T}_{VV}(p^{2})}\Big)+C_{VV}(p^{2})

(4.42)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\Pi}^{T}_{VV}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{9(4\pi)^{2}e^{2}v^{4}m_{h}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{-18m^{4}(m_{h}^{4}+m^{4})+9m_{h}^{2}p^{4}(m_{h}^{2}+m^{2})

(4.43)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{2}\big[2(-2m^{2}p^{2}-m^{4})(m_{h}^{2}-5m^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle p^{2}(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2})^{2}-2m^{2}p^{2}-m^{4}\big]\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}(1-x)+m^{2}x+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2m_{h}^{2}p^{6}+3m_{h}^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\big[p^{2}(m_{h}^{2}+11m^{2})+6m^{4}-p^{4}\big]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3m^{2}\big[-m_{h}^{2}p^{4}+p^{2}(-m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{2}m^{2}+36m^{4})+18m^{6}\big]\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3p^{2}(m_{h}^{6}+10m_{h}^{4}m^{2}+m_{h}^{2}m^{4}+12m^{6})\Bigg\},

(4.44)

and

\displaystyle C_{VV}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{12(4\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{(2m_{h}^{2}+p^{2})\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}(1-x)+m^{2}x+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}.

(4.45)

The resummed form factor (
4.42
) is depicted in Figure
4.4
, as well as the modified version (
4.39
) and their second derivative, which is imported for the spectral analysis in section
C.3
.

Figure 4.4:
Resummed form factor for the vector composite operator (left), the resummed form factor with one subtraction (middle), and the first derivative of the form factor (left).
The momentum
p
is given in units of the energy scale
\mu
, for the parameter values
e=1
,
v=1\,\mu
,
\lambda=\frac{1}{5}
.

Figure 4.5:
Resummed form factor for the vector composite operator.
The momentum
p
is given in units of the energy scale
\mu
, for the parameter values
e=1
,
v=1\,\mu
,
\lambda=\frac{1}{5}
.

Figure 4.6:
Second derivative of the form factor.
The momentum
p
is given in units of the energy scale
\mu
, for the parameter values
e=1
,
v=1\,\mu
,
\lambda=\frac{1}{5}
.

For the longitudinal part of the propagator (see appendix
B.7
for details) , we find the divergent part

\displaystyle\langle V^{\prime}(p)V^{\prime}(-p)\rangle^{L}_{div}

\displaystyle\overset{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}{=}

\displaystyle-\frac{v^{2}\left(3e^{4}+\lambda^{2}\right)}{(4\pi)^{2}\lambda\epsilon}

(4.46)

and the total finite correction up to first order in
\hbar
is given by

\displaystyle\langle V^{\prime}(p)V^{\prime}(-p)\rangle^{L}_{fin}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v^{2}-\Bigg(\frac{m_{h}^{4}-m_{h}^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+m^{4}-3m^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)}{32\pi^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\Bigg)

which means the longitudinal part of the propagator
\braket{V^{\prime}_{\mu}(x),V^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)}
is not propagating.

4.2
Spectral properties of the gauge-invariant local operators
(V_{\mu}(x),O(x))

In this section, we will calculate the spectral properties associated with the correlation function obtained in the last section. In
4.2.1
, we will shortly review the techniques employed in the last chapter to obtain the pole mass, residue and spectral density up to first order in
\hbar
. In
4.2.2
, the spectral properties of the composite operators
(V_{\mu}(x),O(x))
are discussed.

4.2.1
Obtaining the spectral function

For elementary fields we obtained the spectral density function by comparing the Källén-Lehmann spectral representation for the propagator of a generic field
\widetilde{O}(p)

\displaystyle\braket{\widetilde{O}(p)\widetilde{O}(-p)}=G(p^{2})=\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\frac{\rho(t)}{t+p^{2}},

(4.48)

where
\rho(t)
is the spectral density function and
G(p^{2})
stands for the resummed propagator

\displaystyle G(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}-\Pi(p^{2})}.

(4.49)

For higher-dimensional operators, the resummed propagator acquires an overall (dimensionful) factor identical to the one appearing in its tree level result, as we have seen in section
4.1
.
We also note that in the case of higher dimensional operators, the spectral representation,
eq.(
4.48
), might require appropriate subtraction terms in order to ensure convergence. A standard way to cure this problem is to
subtract from
G(p^{2})
the first few terms of its Taylor expansion at
p=0
(
??
), making the integral more and more convergent. These subtraction terms are directly related to the renormalization of the composite operators, and one can see that the modified Green’s functions for the composite scalar field (
4.15
) and for the composite vector field (
4.39
) are in fact subtractions of the Taylor series to first and second order, respectively. In our theory we can make use of the subtracted equations at
p=0
because all fields are massive in the
R_{\xi}
-gauge, so there are no divergences at zero momentum. Also, we stress that the spectral function
\rho(t)
is not affected by the subtraction procedure. Moreover, we can see that these subtractions do not have an influence on the (second) derivative of the propagator. For the scalar composite operator

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(G_{OO}(p^{2})-G_{OO}(0))}{\partial p^{2}}=\frac{\partial G_{OO}(p^{2})}{\partial p^{2}}=-\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\frac{\rho(t)}{(t+p^{2})^{2}},

(4.50)

which means that for a positive spectral function the first derivative of
G_{OO}
is strictly negative, as is confirmed in Figure
4.3
. For the vector composite operator

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}(G_{VV}(p^{2})-G_{VV}(0)-p^{2}G^{\prime}_{VV}(0))}{(\partial p^{2})^{2}}=\frac{\partial^{2}G_{VV}(p^{2})}{(\partial p^{2})^{2}}=2\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\frac{\rho(t)}{(t+p^{2})^{3}},

(4.51)

which should be strictly positive for a positive spectral function, as is shown in Figure
4.6
. Remember, however, that we can also obtain the spectral function directly, by the methods developed in section (
3.3.2
).

4.2.2
Spectral properties of the gauge-invariant composite operators
V_{\mu}(x)
and
O(x)

For the scalar composite operator
{O}(x)
with two-point function given by expression (
4.20
), we find the first-order pole mass for our set of parameter values to be

\displaystyle m^{2}_{h,pole}=0.213472\,\mu^{2},

(4.52)

which is exactly equal to the pole mass of the elementary Higgs field correlator. Following the steps from
3.3.1
, we find the first-order residue to correct the tree-level result
Z_{\text{tree}}=v^{2}
by
\sim 7\%
:

\displaystyle Z=v^{2}(1+\partial_{p^{2}}\Pi_{OO}(p^{2})_{p^{2}=-m_{h}^{2}})=1.06577v^{2}\,,

(4.53)

while the first-order spectral function is shown in Figure
4.7
. Similarly as for the spectral function of the Higgs field in Figure
3.7
, one finds a two-particle threshold for Higgs pair production at
t=(m_{h}+m_{h})^{2}=0.8\,\mu^{2}
, and a two-photon state starting at
t=(m+m)^{2}=4\,\mu^{2}
. The difference is that for this gauge-invariant correlation function we no longer have the unphysical Goldstone two-particle state. Due to the absence of this negative contribution, the spectral function is always positive. Therefore, this quantity is suitable for describing a physical Higgs excitation spectrum as opposed to the elementary propagator
\langle hh\rangle
.

Finally, it is interesting to note that below the unphysical threshold the elementary correlator displays the same qualitative spectral properties as this gauge-invariant approach. This means that spectral description of the physical Higgs mode could in principle be successfully encoded in the elementary propagator in the unitary gauge, in which
\xi\to\infty
and the Goldstone bosons are infinitely heavy. We shall make an explicit comparison in section
4.3
.

Figure 4.7:
Spectral function for the reduced propagator of the scalar composite operator,
\langle{O}(p){O}(-p)\rangle
, with
t
given in units of
\mu^{2}
, for the parameter values
e=1
,
v=1\,\mu
,
\lambda=\frac{1}{5}
.

For the transverse vector composite operator
V^{T}_{\mu}(x)
, with our set of parameters we find the first-order pole mass

\displaystyle m^{2}_{pole}=1.05417\mu^{2},

(4.54)

which is – as expected from the Nielsen identities – exactly the same as the pole mass of the transverse photon field correlator (
3.75
). Furthermore, we find the first-order residue

\displaystyle Z=\frac{e^{2}v^{4}}{4}(1+\partial_{p^{2}}\Pi_{VV}^{T}(p^{2})_{p^{2}=-m^{2}})=1.09332\frac{e^{2}v^{4}}{4}\,,

(4.55)

and the first order spectral density for the reduced propagator is displayed in Figure
4.8
. Like the photon spectral density in Figure
3.6
, we find a photon-Higgs two-particle state at
t=(m_{h}+m)^{2}=2.09\,\mu^{2}
, and the spectral density is positive for all values of
t
.

Figure 4.8:
Spectral function for reduced transverse propagator of the vector composite operator
\langle{V}(p){V}(-p)\rangle^{T}
, with
t
given in units of
\mu^{2}
, for the parameter values
e=1
,
v=1\,\mu
,
\lambda=\frac{1}{5}
.

4.2.3
Pole masses

We can explain the fact that the pole mass of the elementary propagator equals that of its gauge-invariant extension in a qualitative way by looking at the definition of a first-order pole mass, eq. (
3.59
). When calculating one-loop corrections to the two-point function of the composite operators
O(p)
and
R_{\mu}^{a}(p)
, we find that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{c}omposite}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{e}lementary}(p^{2})+\Pi_{\mathrm{1}-leg}(p^{2})(p^{2}+m^{2})+\Pi_{\mathrm{0}-leg}(p^{2})(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2},

(4.56)

where
\Pi_{\mathrm{1}-leg}(p^{2})
and
\Pi_{\mathrm{0}-leg}(p^{2})
are the composite one-loop contributions to the correction of the composite field’s two-point functions, with one external leg and zero external legs, respectively. From this, we see immediately that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{c}omposite}(-m^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{e}lementary}(-m^{2})

(4.57)

and therefore, up to first order in
\hbar
, we find

\displaystyle m^{2}_{\mathrm{p}ole,composite}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m^{2}_{\mathrm{p}ole,elementary}

(4.58)

which means that the elementary operators and their composite extensions share the same mass. This is an important feature, providing an alternative way to the Nielsen identities, to understand why the pole masses of the elementary particles are gauge invariant and not just gauge parameter independent.

4.3
Unitary limit

It is well-known (
??
) that for the Higgs model, the unitary gauge represents the physical gauge, as it decouples the unphysical fields, i.e. the ghost field and the Goldstone field. The unitary gauge can be formally obtained in the
R_{\xi}
-gauges by taking
\xi\rightarrow\infty
. However, this gauge is non-renormalizable, as one can see by looking at this limit for the tree-level propagator of the photon field

\displaystyle\langle A_{\mu}(p)A_{v}(-p)\rangle_{\text{tree}}

\displaystyle\overset{\xi\rightarrow\infty}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}}{\mathcal{P}}_{\mu v}+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\mu v}.

(4.59)

Nonetheless, we can approximate the unitary gauge by taking large values of
\xi
. This is especially interesting when looking at the spectral function of the elementary Higgs field, which is
\xi
-dependent. In Figure
4.9
one finds the spectral function for
\xi=1000
for small and large ranges of
t
. In Figure
4.10
we show the spectral function of the scalar composite field
O(x)
for the same ranges of
t
. As one can see, the pictures are qualitatively very similar. This means that when approximating the unitary gauge, the spectral function of the gauge-dependent, elementary field
h(x)
approximates that of its composite, gauge-invariant counterpart.

Figure 4.9:
Spectral function for the reduced elementary propagator
\langle h(p)h(-p)\rangle
for small values of
t
(left) and large values of
t
(right), with
t
given in units of
\mu^{2}
, for
\xi=1000
the parameter values
e=1
,
v=1\,\mu
,
\lambda=\frac{1}{5}
.

Figure 4.10:
Spectral function for the reduced composite propagator
\langle O(p),O(-p)\rangle
for small values of
t
(left) and large values of
t
(right), with
t
given in units of
\mu^{2}
, for the parameter values
e=1
,
v=1\,\mu
,
\lambda=\frac{1}{5}
.

4.4
Conclusion

In the present chapter, following the local gauge-invariant setup of (
????????
), we have evaluated at one-loop order the two-point correlation functions
\langle V^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)V^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)\rangle
,
\langle O(x)O(y)\rangle
of the two local gauge-invariant operators
{V}^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)=-i\varphi^{\dagger}(x)D_{\mu}\varphi(x)+i\partial_{\mu}O(x)
and
{O}(x)=\varphi^{\dagger}(x)\varphi(x)-\frac{v}{2}
in the
U(1)
Abelian Higgs model quantized in the
R_{\xi}
gauge. Our results can be summarized as follows:

•

both
\langle V^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)V^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)\rangle
and
\langle O(x)O(y)\rangle
do not depend on the gauge parameter
\xi
, as expected;

•

the pole masses of
\langle V^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)V^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)\rangle^{T}
and
\langle O(x)O(y)\rangle
are exactly the same as those of the correlation functions of the elementary fields
\langle A_{\mu}(x)A_{\nu}(y)\rangle^{T}
and
\langle h(x)h(y)\rangle
, respectively, where
\langle\cdots\rangle_{T}
stands for the transverse component of the corresponding propagator;

•

the spectral densities of the Källén-Lehmann representation of the correlation functions
\langle V^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)V^{\prime}_{\nu}(y)\rangle
and
\langle O(x)O(y)\rangle
turn out to be always positive, in contrast to the one associated with the (gauge-dependent) elementary Higgs field.

These important features give us a fully gauge-invariant picture in order to describe the spectrum of elementary excitations of the model, i.e. the massive photon and the Higgs mode.

Chapter 5
Spectral properties of local BRST invariant composite operators in the
SU(2)
Yang-Mills-Higgs model

In this chapter we will extend the techniques of chapter

3
and
4
to the more complex case of
SU(2)
Higgs model with a single Higgs field in the fundamental representation. This model is equal to the electroweak model from chapter
1.1
with the coupling constant of the hypercharge interaction taken to zero,
g^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
. As we have seen for the Abelian gauge theory, the direct use of non-gauge invariant fields displays several limitations in the spectral representation, and this will become more severe in the case of a non-Abelian gauge theory. For instance, in the case of the
U(1)
Higgs model, the transverse component of the Abelian gauge field
A_{\mu}
is gauge invariant, so that the two-point correlation function

\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)\langle A_{\mu}(p)A_{\nu}(-p)\rangle
, where
\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)=(\delta_{\mu\nu}-\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{2}})
is the transverse projector, turns out to be independent from the gauge parameter
\xi
. However, this is no more true in the non-Abelian case, where both Higgs and gauge boson two-point functions,
i.e.

\braket{h(p)h(-p)}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)\langle A^{a}_{\mu}(p)A^{b}_{\nu}(-p)\rangle
, where
h
stands for the Higgs field and
A^{a}_{\mu}
for the gauge boson field, exhibit a strong gauge dependence from
\xi
. As a consequence, the understanding of the two-point correlation functions of both Higgs field
h
and gauge vector boson
A^{a}_{\mu}
in terms of the Källén-Lehmann (KL) spectral representation is completely jeopardized by an unphysical dependence from the gauge parameter
\xi
, obscuring a direct interpretation of the above mentioned correlation functions in terms of the elementary excitations of the physical spectrum, namely the Higgs and the vector gauge boson particles.

We also note here that from a lattice perspective, it is expected that the spectrum of a gauge (Higgs) theory should be describable in terms of local gauge invariant operator correlation functions, with concrete physical information hiding in the various (positive and gauge invariant) spectral functions, not only pole masses, decay widths, but also transport coefficients at finite temperature etc. Clearly, such information will not correctly be encoded in gauge variant, non-positive spectral functions.

As we discussed in section
1.1.4
, besides the exact BRST invariance, the quantized theory exhibits a global
SU(2)
symmetry known as custodial symmetry. In this chapter we will show how the local composite BRST invariant operators corresponding to the gauge bosons transform as a triplet under the custodial symmetry, a property which will imply useful relations for their two-point correlation functions.

This chapter is organized as follows. In section
5.1
, we give a review of the
SU(2)
HYM model with a single Higgs field in the fundamental representation, of the gauge fixing procedure and its ensuing BRST invariance. In section
5.2
we calculate the two-point correlation functions of the elementary fields up to one-loop order. In section
5.3
, we define the BRST invariant local composite operators
(O(x),R^{a}_{\mu}(x))
corresponding to the BRST invariant extension of
(h,A^{a}_{\mu})
and calculate their one-loop correlation functions. In section
5.4
, we discuss the spectral properties of both elementary and composite operators. In order to give a more general idea of the behavior of the spectral functions, we shall be using two sets of parameters which we shall refer as to Region I and Region II. To some extent, Region I can be associated to the perturbative weak coupling regime, while in Region II we keep the gauge coupling a little bit larger, while decreasing the minimizing value
v
of the Higgs field. Section
5.5
is devoted to our conclusion. The technical details are all collected in the appendices.

5.1
The action and its symmetries

The YM action with a single Higgs field in the fundamental representation is given by

\displaystyle S_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\left\{\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}+(D_{\mu}^{ij}\Phi^{\dagger j})(D_{\mu}^{ik}\Phi^{k})+\frac{\lambda}{2}(\Phi^{\dagger i}\Phi^{i}-\frac{1}{2}v^{2})^{2}\right\}

(5.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{\mathrm{Y}M}+S_{\mathrm{H}iggs}

with

\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}^{a}+g\epsilon^{abc}A_{\mu}^{b}A_{\nu}^{c}

(5.2)

and

\displaystyle D_{\mu}^{ij}\Phi^{j}=\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i}-\frac{i}{2}g(\tau^{a})^{ij}A_{\mu}^{a}\Phi^{j},\,\,\,(D_{\mu}^{ij}\Phi^{j})^{\dagger}=\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{i\dagger}+\frac{i}{2}g\Phi^{j\dagger}(\tau^{a})^{ji}A_{\mu}^{a},

(5.3)

with the Pauli matrices
\tau^{a}(a=1,2,3)
and the Levi-Civita tensor
\epsilon^{abc}
referring to the gauge symmetry group
SU(2)
. The scalar complex field
\Phi^{i}(x)
is in the fundamental representation of
SU(2)
,

i.e.

i,j=1,2
. Thus,
\Phi
is an
SU(2)
-doublet of complex scalar fields that can be written as

\displaystyle\Phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\phi^{+}\\
\phi^{0}\end{pmatrix}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{1}+i\phi_{2}\\
\phi_{3}+i\phi_{4}\end{pmatrix}.

(5.4)

The configuration which minimizes the Higgs potential in the expression (
5.1
) is

\displaystyle\braket{\Phi}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}v\\
0\end{pmatrix}

(5.5)

and we write down
\Phi(x)
as an expansion around the configuration (
5.5
), so that

\displaystyle\Phi=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}v+h+i\rho_{3}\\
i\rho_{1}-\rho_{2}\end{pmatrix},

(5.6)

where
h
is the Higgs field and
\rho^{a}
,
a=1,2,3
, the would-be Goldstone bosons. We can use the matrix notation
1
1

1

This is of course possible thanks to the fact that
\Phi
counts 3 Goldstone modes and that
SU(2)
has three generators. This “numerology” is essentially what leads to a large custodial symmetry in the
SU(2)
case.
:

\displaystyle\Phi=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}((v+h)\textbf{1}+i\rho^{a}\tau^{a})\cdot\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix},

(5.7)

so that the second term in eq. (
5.1
) becomes

\displaystyle(D^{ij}_{\mu}\Phi^{j})^{\dagger}D_{\mu}^{ik}\Phi^{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(1,0)\cdot\Bigg[\partial_{\mu}h\cdot\textbf{1}-i\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}\tau^{a}+\frac{ig}{2}\tau^{a}A_{\mu}^{a}\Big((v+h)\textbf{1}

(5.8)

\displaystyle-i\rho^{b}\tau^{b}\Big)\Bigg]\times\Bigg[\partial_{\mu}h\cdot\textbf{1}+i\partial_{\mu}\rho^{c}\tau^{c}-\frac{ig}{2}((v+h)\textbf{1}

\displaystyle-i\rho^{d}\tau^{d}\Big)\tau^{c}A_{\mu}^{c}\Bigg]\cdot\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}_{0}}+\tilde{\mathcal{L}_{1}}+\tilde{\mathcal{L}_{2}},

with
\tilde{\mathcal{L}_{i}}
the
i
th term in powers of
A_{\mu}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left((\partial_{\mu}h)^{2}+\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left\{gvA_{\mu}^{a}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}-gA_{\mu}^{a}\rho^{a}\partial_{\mu}h+gA_{\mu}^{a}(\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a})h+g\epsilon^{abc}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}\rho^{b}A_{\mu}^{c}\right\},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{g^{2}}{8}A_{\mu}^{a}A_{\mu}^{a}\left[(v+h)^{2}+\rho^{b}\rho^{b}\right],

(5.9)

and we have the full action

\displaystyle S_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\frac{1}{2}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{2}F^{a}_{\mu\nu}F^{a}_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{4}v^{2}g^{2}A_{\mu}^{a}A_{\mu}^{a}+(\partial_{\mu}h)^{2}+\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}-gvA_{\mu}^{a}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}+gA_{\mu}^{a}\rho^{a}\partial_{\mu}h-gA^{a}_{\mu}(\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a})h

(5.10)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle g\epsilon^{abc}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}\rho^{b}A_{\mu}^{c}+\frac{g^{2}}{4}A_{\mu}^{a}A_{\mu}^{a}\left[2vh+h^{2}+\rho^{b}\rho^{b}\right]+\lambda v^{2}h^{2}+\lambda vh(h^{2}+\rho^{a}\rho^{a})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{4}(h^{2}+\rho^{a}\rho^{a})^{2}\Bigg\}.

One sees that both gauge field
A^{a}_{\mu}
and Higgs field
h
have acquired a mass given, respectively, by

\displaystyle m^{2}=\frac{1}{4}g^{2}v^{2},\,\,\,\,\,\,\,m_{h}^{2}=\lambda v^{2}\;.

(5.11)

5.1.1
Gauge fixing and BRST symmetry

The action (
5.1
) is invariant under the local
\omega
-parametrized gauge transformations

\delta A_{\mu}^{a}=-D_{\mu}^{ab}\omega^{b},\,\,\,\delta\Phi=-\frac{ig}{2}\omega^{a}\tau^{a}\Phi,\,\,\,\delta\Phi^{\dagger}=\frac{ig}{2}\omega^{a}\Phi^{\dagger}\tau^{a},

(5.12)

which, when written in terms of the fields
(h,\rho^{a})
, become

\delta h=\frac{g}{2}\omega^{a}\rho^{a},\,\,\,\delta\rho^{a}=-\frac{g}{2}(\omega^{a}(v+h)\textbf{1}-\epsilon^{abc}\omega^{b}\rho^{c}).

(5.13)

As done in the
U(1)
case, we shall be using the
R_{\xi}
-gauge. We add thus need the gauge fixing term

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{g}f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s\int d^{4}x\Bigl\{-i\frac{\xi}{2}\bar{c}^{a}b^{a}+\bar{c}^{a}(\partial_{\mu}A_{\mu}^{a}-\xi m\rho^{a})\Bigr\}

(5.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int d^{4}x\Bigl\{\xi b^{a}b^{a}+2ib^{a}\partial_{\mu}A_{\mu}^{a}+2\bar{c}^{a}\partial_{\mu}D_{\mu}^{ab}c^{b}-2i\xi mb^{a}\rho^{a}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2\xi\bar{c}^{a}mc^{a}-g\xi\bar{c}^{a}mhc^{a}-\xi g\epsilon^{abc}\bar{c}^{a}c^{b}\rho^{c}\Bigr\}\;,

so that the gauge fixed action
S_{\text{full}}=S_{0}+\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{g}f}
, namely

\displaystyle S_{\text{full}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{4}x\frac{1}{2}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{2}F^{a}_{\mu\nu}F^{a}_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{4}v^{2}g^{2}A_{\mu}^{a}A_{\mu}^{a}

(5.15)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\partial_{\mu}h)^{2}+\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}-gvA_{\mu}^{a}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}+gA_{\mu}^{a}\rho^{a}\partial_{\mu}h-gA^{a}_{\mu}(\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a})h

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle g\epsilon^{abc}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}\rho^{b}A_{\mu}^{c}+\frac{g^{2}}{4}A_{\mu}^{a}A_{\mu}^{a}\left[2vh+h^{2}+\rho^{b}\rho^{b}\right]+\lambda v^{2}h^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lambda vh(h^{2}+\rho^{a}\rho^{a})+\frac{\lambda}{4}(h^{2}+\rho^{a}\rho^{a})^{2}+\xi b^{a}b^{a}+2ib^{a}\partial_{\mu}A_{\mu}^{a}+2\bar{c}^{a}\partial_{\mu}D_{\mu}^{ab}c^{b}-2i\xi mb^{a}\rho^{a}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2\xi\bar{c}^{a}mc^{a}-g\xi\bar{c}^{a}mhc^{a}-\xi g\epsilon^{abc}\bar{c}^{a}c^{b}\rho^{c}\Bigg\}

turns out to be left invariant by the BRST transformations

\displaystyle sA_{\mu}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-D_{\mu}^{ab}c^{b},\,\,\,sh=\frac{g}{2}c^{a}\rho^{a},\,\,\,s\rho^{a}=-\frac{g}{2}(c^{a}(v+h)\textbf{1}-\epsilon^{abc}c^{b}\rho^{c})

\displaystyle sc^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g\epsilon^{abc}c^{b}c^{c},\,\,\,s\bar{c}^{a}=ib^{a},\,\,\,sb^{a}=0\;,

(5.16)

sS_{\text{full}}=0\;.

(5.17)

5.1.2
Custodial symmetry

As already mentioned, apart from the BRST symmetry, there is an extra global symmetry, which we shall refer to as the custodial symmetry:

\displaystyle\delta A^{a}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{abc}\beta^{b}A^{c}_{\mu},

\displaystyle\delta\rho^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{abc}\beta^{b}\rho^{c},

\displaystyle\delta\overline{c}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{abc}\beta^{b}\overline{c}^{c},

\displaystyle\delta c^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{abc}\beta^{b}c^{c},

\displaystyle\delta b^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{abc}\beta^{b}b^{c},

\displaystyle\delta h

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\;,

(5.18)

where
\beta^{a}
is a constant parameter,
\partial_{\mu}\beta^{a}=0
,

\delta S_{\text{full}}=0\;.

(5.19)

One notices that all fields carrying the index
a=1,2,3
,
i.e.

(A^{a}_{\mu},b^{a},c^{a},{\bar{c}}^{a},\rho^{a})
, undergo a global transformation in the adjoint representation of
SU(2)
. The origin of this symmetry is an
SU(2)_{\mathrm{g}auge}\times SU(2)_{\mathrm{g}lobal}
symmetry of the action in the unbroken phase, see section
1.1.4
. The exception is the Higgs field
h
, which is left invariant,
i.e.
it is a singlet. As we shall see in the following, this additional global symmetry will provide useful relationships for the two-point correlation functions of the BRST invariant composite operators.

5.2
One-loop evaluation of the
correlation function of the elementary fields

For the elementary fields
h(x)
and
A^{a}_{\mu}
, the correlation functions are calculated in Appendices
C.2.1
and
C.2.2
. For the Higgs field, for the one-loop propagator we get

\displaystyle\braket{h(p)h(-p)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p^{2}+m_{h}^{2}}+\frac{1}{(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})^{2}}\Pi_{hh}(p^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2})

(5.20)

where

\displaystyle\Pi_{hh}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3g^{2}}{{8(4\pi)^{2}}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\Bigg\{2\xi\left(m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}\xi}{\mu^{2}}\right)-2\xi m_{h}^{2}+2\left(6m^{2}-p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

(5.21)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(12m^{2}+\frac{p^{4}}{m^{2}}+4p^{2})\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)+\left(\frac{p^{4}}{m^{2}}-\frac{m_{h}^{4}}{m^{2}}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}\xi+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 12m^{2}-2\xi p^{2}+2p^{2}-\frac{m_{h}^{4}}{m^{2}}\left(-2\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+3\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)+2\right)\Bigg\}.

Before trying to resum the self-energy
\Pi_{hh}(p^{2})
, we notice that this resummation is tacitly assuming that the
second term in (
5.20
) is much smaller than the first term. However, we see that eq. (
5.20
) contains terms of the order of
\frac{p^{4}}{(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})^{2}}\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)
which cannot be resummed for big values of
p^{2}
. We therefore proceed as in eq. (
3.51
)-(
3.53
) and use the identity

\displaystyle p^{4}=(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})^{2}-m_{h}^{4}-2p^{2}m_{h}^{2}

(5.22)

to rewrite

\displaystyle\frac{p^{4}}{(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})^{2}}\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}-\underline{\frac{(m_{h}^{4}+2p^{2}m_{h}^{2})}{(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})^{2}}\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}}.

(5.23)

The term which has been underlined in eq. (
5.23
) can be safely resummed, as it decays fast enough for large values of
p^{2}
. We thence rewrite

\displaystyle\frac{{\Pi}_{hh}(p^{2})}{(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{\Pi}_{hh}(p^{2})}{(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})^{2}}+C_{hh}(p^{2})\;,

(5.24)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\Pi}_{hh}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3g^{2}}{{8(4\pi)^{2}}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\,\Bigg\{2\xi\left(m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}\xi}{\mu^{2}}\right)-2\xi m_{h}^{2}+2\left(6m^{2}-p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

(5.25)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(12m^{2}-\frac{(m_{h}^{4}+2p^{2}m_{h}^{2})}{m^{2}}+4p^{2})\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)-\frac{(2m_{h}^{4}+2p^{2}m_{h}^{2})}{m^{2}}\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}\xi+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 12m^{2}-2\xi p^{2}+2p^{2}-\frac{m_{h}^{4}}{m^{2}}\Big(-2\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+3\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)+2\Big)\Bigg\}

and

\displaystyle C_{hh}(p^{2})=-\frac{3g^{2}}{{8m^{2}(4\pi)^{2}}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\left(\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}-\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+\xi m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\;.

(5.26)

Thus, for the one-loop Higgs propagator, we get

\displaystyle\braket{h(p)h(-p)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p^{2}+m_{h}^{2}-\hat{\Pi}_{hh}(p^{2})}+C_{hh}(p^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2})\;.

(5.27)

For the gauge field, we split the
two-point function into transverse and longitudinal parts in the usual way

\displaystyle\braket{A_{\mu}^{a}(p)A^{b}_{\nu}(-p)}=\braket{A_{\mu}^{a}(p)A^{b}_{\nu}(-p)}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)+\braket{A_{\mu}^{a}(p)A^{b}_{\nu}(-p)}^{L}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}(p),

(5.28)

where we have introduced the transverse and longitudinal projectors, given respectively by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu\nu}-\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{2}}\,,\qquad\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}(p)~=~\frac{p_{\mu}p_{\nu}}{p^{2}}\,.

(5.29)

We find

\displaystyle\braket{A_{\mu}^{a}(p)A_{\nu}^{b}(-p)}^{T}=\frac{\delta^{ab}}{p^{2}+m^{2}}+\frac{\delta^{ab}}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\Pi_{AA^{T}}(p^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2}),

(5.30)

\displaystyle\Pi_{AA^{T}}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta^{ab}g^{2}}{36(4\pi)^{2}m^{4}p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{-27m^{4}p^{2}m_{h}^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)-27m^{6}\xi p^{2}m_{h}^{2}\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}\xi}{\mu^{2}}\right)

(5.31)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3m^{4}m_{h}^{4}\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}+2p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+27m^{4}p^{2}m_{h}^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}+m^{2}\xi\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m^{4}\xi m_{h}^{2}\left(2m^{4}(\xi-1)+m^{2}(4\xi+7)p^{2}+2(\xi+9)p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}\xi}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3m^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\left(-m^{2}m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{2}\left(m^{4}(2\xi-1)+m^{2}(4\xi+45)p^{2}+2(\xi+9)p^{4}\right)-54m^{4}p^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m^{4}\Big(6m^{2}m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{2}\left(3m^{4}(2(\xi-2)\xi+1)+3m^{2}(\xi-1)(4\xi-1)p^{2}+2(3\xi(\xi+4)-17)p^{4}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{6}+54m^{4}p^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{2}\Big[m^{4}\left(2p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}-5m^{2}\right)+\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}\right){}^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+m_{h}^{2}(1-x)+m^{2}x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2\left(m^{2}+p^{2}\right)^{2}\left(m^{4}(\xi-1)^{2}+2m^{2}(\xi-5)p^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+\xi m^{2}(1-x)+m^{2}x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle p^{2}\left(p^{4}-m^{4}\right)\left(4m^{2}\xi+p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+\xi m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle p^{2}\left(4m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\left(12m^{4}-20m^{2}p^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Big]\Bigg\}.

We see that (
5.30
) contains again terms of the order
\frac{p^{4}}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)
and
\frac{p^{6}}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)
, which cannot be resummed for big values of
p^{2}
. We use

\displaystyle\frac{p^{4}}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}-\underline{\frac{(m^{4}+2p^{2}m^{2})}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}}

(5.32)

and

\displaystyle\frac{p^{6}}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(p^{2}-2m^{2})\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}

(5.33)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underline{\frac{2m^{6}+3p^{2}m^{4}}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\ln\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}}.

The underlined terms in (
5.32
) and (
5.33
) can be safely resummed. We rewrite

\displaystyle\frac{{\Pi}_{AA^{T}}(p^{2})}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{\Pi}_{AA^{T}}(p^{2})}{(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})^{2}}+C_{AA^{T}}(p^{2}),

(5.34)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\Pi}_{AA^{T}}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta^{ab}g^{2}}{36(4\pi)^{2}m^{4}p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{-27m^{4}p^{2}m_{h}^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)-27m^{6}\xi p^{2}m_{h}^{2}\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}\xi}{\mu^{2}}\right)

(5.35)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3m^{4}m_{h}^{4}\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}+2p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+27m^{4}p^{2}m_{h}^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}+m^{2}\xi\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m^{4}\xi m_{h}^{2}\left(2m^{4}(\xi-1)+m^{2}(4\xi+7)p^{2}+2(\xi+9)p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}\xi}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3m^{4}\left(-m^{2}m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{2}\left(m^{4}(2\xi-1)+m^{2}(4\xi+45)p^{2}+2(\xi+9)p^{4}\right)-54m^{4}p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m^{4}\left(6m^{2}m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{2}\left(3m^{4}(2(\xi-2)\xi+1)+3m^{2}(\xi-1)(4\xi-1)p^{2}+2(3\xi(\xi+4)-17)p^{4}\right)-3m_{h}^{6}+54m^{4}p^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{2}\Big[m^{4}\left(2p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}-5m^{2}\right)+\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}\right){}^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+(1-x)m_{h}^{2}+m^{2}x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2m^{4}(\xi-1)^{2}\left(m^{2}+p^{2}\right)^{2}\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+\xi m^{2}(1-x)+m^{2}x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(-2m^{4}(4\xi-1)p^{2}\left(m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+\xi m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(66m^{6}p^{2}-33m^{4}p^{4})\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Big]\Bigg\}.

and

\displaystyle C_{AA^{T}}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{ab}g^{2}}{12(4\pi)^{2}m^{4}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{(-4m^{2}(\xi-5)-2p^{2})

(5.36)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+\xi m^{2}(1-x)+m^{2}x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(4\,\xi m^{2}+p^{2}-2m^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+\xi m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(-18m^{2}+p^{2})\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}.

Finally

\displaystyle\braket{A_{\mu}^{a}(p)A_{\nu}^{b}(-p)}^{T}=\delta^{ab}\left(\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}-\hat{\Pi}_{AA^{T}}(p^{2})}+C_{AA^{T}}(p^{2})\right)+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2})\;.

(5.37)

5.3
One-loop evaluation of the
correlation function of the local BRST invariant composite operators

5.3.1
Correlation function of the scalar BRST invariant composite operator
O(x)

The BRST invariant local scalar composite operator
O(x)
is given by

O(x)=\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi-\frac{v^{2}}{2}\;,\qquad s\;O(x)=0\;,

(5.38)

which, after using the expansion (
5.7
), becomes

\displaystyle O(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big[\begin{pmatrix}1&0\end{pmatrix}((v+h)\textbf{1}-i\rho^{a}\tau^{a}))((v+h)\textbf{1}+i\rho^{b}\tau^{b}))\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}\Big]-\frac{v^{2}}{2}

(5.39)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big(h^{2}(x)+2vh(x)+\rho^{a}(x)\rho^{a}(x)\Big)\;,

so that

\displaystyle\braket{{O}(x){O}(y)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v^{2}\braket{h(x)h(y)}+v\braket{h(x)\rho^{b}(y)\rho^{b}(y)}+v\braket{h(x)h(y)^{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\braket{h(x)^{2}\rho^{b}(y)\rho^{b}(y)}+\frac{1}{4}\braket{h(x)^{2}h(y)^{2}}

(5.40)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\braket{\rho^{a}(x)\rho^{a}(x)\rho^{b}(y)\rho^{b}(y)}.

Looking at the tree level expression of eq. (
5.40
), one easily obtains

\displaystyle\braket{{O}(p){O}(-p)}_{\mathrm{t}ree}=v^{2}\braket{h(p)h(-p)}_{\mathrm{t}ree}=v^{2}\frac{1}{p^{2}+m_{h}^{2}}\;,

(5.41)

showing that the BRST invariant scalar operator
O(x)
is directly linked to the Higgs propagator.

Concerning now the one-loop calculation of expression (
5.40
), after evaluating each term, see Appendix
C.3
for details, we find that the two-point correlation function of the scalar composite operator
O(x)
develops a geometric series in the same way as the elementary field
h(x)
. This allows us the make a resummed approximation. Using dimensional regularization in the
\overline{MS}bar
-scheme, we find

\displaystyle\braket{O(p)O(-p)}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}}{p^{2}+m_{h}^{2}}+\frac{v^{2}\,}{(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})^{2}}\Pi_{OO}(p^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2}),

(5.42)

\displaystyle\Pi_{OO}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{32v^{2}\pi^{2}m_{h}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{-24m_{h}^{2}m^{4}-6m^{2}p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}+6m^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

(5.43)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle m_{h}^{2}\left(p^{2}-2m_{h}^{2}\right)^{2}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}x(1-x)}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{2}\left(12m^{4}+4m^{2}p^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}x(1-x)}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 6p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{2}m^{2}+2m^{4}\right)-6m_{h}^{4}p^{2}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}.

Since (
5.42
) contains terms of the order of
\frac{p^{4}}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\ln(p^{2})
,
we follow the steps (
3.51
)-(
3.53
) to find the resummed correlation function in the one-loop approximation

\displaystyle G_{{O}{O}}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{2}}{p^{2}+m_{h}^{2}-\hat{\Pi}_{OO}(p^{2})}+C_{OO}(p^{2})

(5.44)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\Pi}_{OO}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{32v^{2}\pi^{2}m_{h}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{-24m_{h}^{2}m^{4}-6m^{2}p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}+6m^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

(5.45)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle m_{h}^{2}(3m_{h}^{4}-6m_{h}^{2}p^{2})\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}x(1-x)}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{2}\left(12m^{4}+4m^{2}p^{2}-m_{h}^{4}-2p^{2}m_{h}^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}x(1-x)}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 6p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{2}m^{2}+2m^{4}\right)-6m_{h}^{4}p^{2}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}

and

\displaystyle C_{OO}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}x(1-x)}{\mu^{2}}\right)+3\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}x(1-x)}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}.

(5.46)

Expressions (
5.44
) and (
5.46
) show that, as expected, and unlike the Higgs propagator, eq. (
5.27
), the correlator
\braket{O(p)O(-p)}
is independent from the gauge parameter
\xi
.

5.3.2
A little digression on the unitary gauge

Of course, since the composite operator
O(x)
is BRST invariant, any choice for the gauge parameter
\xi
should give the same expression for the correlation function
G_{OO}(p^{2})
. One convenient choice is the so-called unitary gauge, which is formally attained by taking
\xi\rightarrow\infty
at the end of the calculation. Though, we take here a different route and perform the same calculation done before for
G_{OO}(p^{2})
by employing the tree level propagators and other Feynman rules which follow by taking the limit
\xi\rightarrow\infty
at the beginning. In doing this, for the tree level propagators one finds

\displaystyle\langle A^{a}_{\mu}(p)A^{b}_{v}(-p)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{ab}}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\mu v}(p)+\delta^{ab}\frac{1}{m^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\mu v}(p)=\frac{\delta^{ab}}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\Big(\delta_{\mu\nu}+\frac{p_{\mu}p_{v}}{m^{2}}\Big),

\displaystyle\langle h(p)h(-p)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p^{2}+m_{h}^{2}}\,

(5.47)

with all other propagators,
i.e.
the Goldstone and Faddeev-Popov ghost propagators, vanishing. Then, eq. (
5.40
) simplifies to

\displaystyle\braket{{O}(x){O}(y)}_{\mathrm{u}nitary}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v^{2}\braket{h(x)h(y)}_{\mathrm{u}nitary}+v\braket{h(x)h(y)^{2}}_{\mathrm{u}nitary}+\frac{1}{4}\braket{h(x)^{2}h(y)^{2}}_{\mathrm{u}nitary},

(5.48)

with the contributing diagrams shown in Figure
5.1
. Making use of the dimensional regularization in the
\overline{MS}bar
-scheme and switching to momentum space, we get

\displaystyle v^{2}\braket{h(p)h(-p)}_{\mathrm{u}nitary}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(2m_{h}^{4}+12m^{2}p^{2}+2p^{4}\right)+2m_{h}^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+2\left(6m^{4}-m^{2}p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

(5.49)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3m_{h}^{4}\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)-\left(12m^{4}+4m^{2}p^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2m_{h}^{4}+2m^{2}\left(p^{2}-6m^{2}\right)\Bigg\}\frac{1}{\left(m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}\right){}^{2}},

\displaystyle v\braket{h(p)h(-p)^{2}}_{\mathrm{u}nitary}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{16\pi^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\frac{12}{\epsilon}m^{4}-6m^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)-m_{h}^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

(5.50)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m_{h}^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)+m_{h}^{4}+2m^{4}\Bigg\}\frac{1}{\left(m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}\right)},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\braket{h(p)^{2}h(p)^{2}}_{\mathrm{u}nitary}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon}-\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}.

(5.51)

Inserting now the unity

\displaystyle 1=(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})/(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})=((p^{2}+m_{h}^{2})/(p^{2}+m_{h}^{2}))^{2},

(5.52)

we find indeed that
\braket{O(x)O(y)}_{\mathrm{u}nitary}=\braket{O(x)O(y)}
, showing that the same expression has been re-obtained by starting directly from the tree level propagators of the unitary gauge,
\xi\rightarrow\infty
, despite the fact that this gauge is known to be non-renormalizable. Though, at one-loop order, a simple explanation can be found for the previous result, which is due in part to the BRST invariant nature of the correlation function
\braket{O(x)O(y)}
and to the fact that, at one-loop order, the handling of the overlapping divergences is not required. From two-loop onward these divergences will show up, requiring a fully renormalizable setup. More specifically, in this case, one would need to keep
\xi
finite and use all Feynman rules of the
R_{\xi}
-gauge, while taking
\xi\rightarrow\infty
only at the end. Nevertheless, having checked that the one-loop result for
G_{OO}(p^{2})
is the same by using both procedures, in the next section, we will use the same simplifying second trick to evaluate the two-point function of the vectorial composite operator at one-loop order.

Figure 5.1:
One-loop contributions for the propagator
\langle O(x)O(y)\rangle
in the unitary gauge:
\braket{h(x)h(y)}
(first line),
\braket{h(x)h(y)^{2}}
(second line) and
\braket{h(x)^{2}h(y)^{2}}
(third line) . Wavy lines represent the gauge field, dashed lines the Higgs field, solid lines the Goldstone boson and double lines the ghost field. The
\bullet
indicates the insertion of a composite operator.

.

5.3.3
Vectorial composite operators

We identify three gauge invariant vector composite operators, following the definitions of ’t Hooft in (
??
), namely

\displaystyle O_{\mu}^{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\phi^{\dagger}D_{\mu}\phi,

\displaystyle O_{\mu}^{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi^{T}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}D_{\mu}\phi,

\displaystyle O_{\mu}^{-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(O_{\mu}^{+})^{\dagger}.

(5.53)

The gauge invariance of
O_{\mu}^{3}
is apparent. For
O^{+}_{\mu}
, we can show the gauge invariance by using the following
2\times 2
matrix representation of a generic
SU(2)
transformation,

\displaystyle U=\begin{pmatrix}a&-b^{\star}\\
b&a^{\star}\end{pmatrix}

(5.54)

with determinant
|a|^{2}+|b|^{2}=1
. Thus, we find that under a
SU(2)
transformation

\displaystyle O_{\mu}^{+}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle(U\phi)^{T}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}D_{\mu}(U\phi)

(5.55)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi^{T}U^{T}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}UD_{\mu}\phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi^{T}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}D_{\mu}\phi=O_{\mu}^{+},

which shows the gauge invariance of
O_{\mu}^{+}
and, subsequently, of
O_{\mu}^{-}
. After using the expansion (
5.7
), the first composite operator reads

\displaystyle O_{\mu}^{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\phi^{\dagger}D_{\mu}\phi

(5.56)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\phi^{\dagger}\partial_{\mu}\phi+\frac{1}{2}g\phi^{\dagger}\tau^{a}A_{\mu}^{a}\phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\Bigg[(v+h)\partial_{\mu}h+i(v+h)\partial_{\mu}\rho^{3}-i\rho^{3}\partial_{\mu}h+\rho^{a}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}+i\rho^{1}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{2}-i\rho^{2}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{1}-\frac{i}{2}g(v+h)^{2}A_{\mu}^{3}+ig(v+h)(A_{\mu}^{1}\rho^{2}-A_{\mu}^{2}\rho^{1})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}g\rho^{a}A_{\mu}^{3}\rho^{a}-ig\rho^{3}A_{\mu}^{b}\rho^{b}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[-(v+h)\partial_{\mu}\rho^{3}+\rho^{3}\partial_{\mu}h-\rho^{1}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{2}+\rho^{2}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{1}+\frac{1}{2}g(v+h)^{2}A_{\mu}^{3}-g(v+h)(A_{\mu}^{1}\rho^{2}-A_{\mu}^{2}\rho^{1})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g\rho^{a}A_{\mu}^{3}\rho^{a}+g\rho^{3}A_{\mu}^{b}\rho^{b}\Bigg]+\frac{i}{2}\partial_{\mu}O,

and since the last term,
i.e.

\frac{i}{2}\partial_{\mu}O
, is BRST invariant, the sum of the others terms has to be BRST invariant too. Therefore, we can introduce the following three “reduced” vector composite operators
R^{a}_{\mu}
with
a=1,2,3
:

\displaystyle R_{\mu}^{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\Big(O_{\mu}^{+}-O^{-}_{\mu}\Big),

\displaystyle R_{\mu}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big(O_{\mu}^{+}+O^{-}_{\mu}\Big),

\displaystyle R_{\mu}^{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{\mu}^{3}-\frac{i}{2}\partial_{\mu}O_{\mu},

(5.57)

so that

\displaystyle R_{\mu}^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[-(v+h)\partial_{\mu}\rho^{a}+\rho^{a}\partial_{\mu}h-\varepsilon^{abc}\rho^{b}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{c}+\frac{1}{2}g(v+h)^{2}A_{\mu}^{a}-g(v+h)\varepsilon^{abc}(\rho^{b}A_{\mu}^{c})

(5.58)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}gA_{\mu}^{a}\rho^{m}\rho^{m}+g\rho^{a}A_{\mu}^{m}\rho^{m}\Bigg]\;,

with

sR^{a}_{\mu}(x)=0\;.

(5.59)

Remarkably, the BRST invariant operators
R_{\mu}^{a}
transform like a triplet under the custodial symmetry (
5.18
), namely

\displaystyle\delta R_{\mu}^{a}=\varepsilon^{abc}\beta^{b}R_{\mu}^{c}\;,

(5.60)

and since the only rank two invariant tensor is
\delta^{ab}
, we can write, moving to momentum space,

\displaystyle\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(p)\,R^{b}_{\nu}(-p)}=\delta^{ab}R_{\mu\nu}(p^{2})\,\,\rightarrow\,\,R_{\mu\nu}(p^{2})=\frac{1}{3}\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(p)\,R^{a}_{\nu}(-p)},

(5.61)

as well as

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}(p^{2})=R(p^{2})\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)+L(p^{2})\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}(p),

(5.62)

so that in
d
dimensions,

\displaystyle R(p^{2})=\frac{1}{3}\frac{\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)}{(d-1)}\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(p)\,R^{a}_{\nu}(-p)},

(5.63)

and

\displaystyle L(p^{2})=\frac{1}{3}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}(p)\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(p)\,R^{a}_{\nu}(-p)}.

(5.64)

One recognizes that eqs.(
5.61
)-(
5.64
) display exactly the same structure of the gauge vector boson correlation function
\langle A^{a}_{\mu}(p)A_{\nu}(-p)\rangle
.

In the
R_{\xi}
-gauge, the non-vanishing contributions, up to first order in
\hbar
, to the correlation function
\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(p)\,R^{b}_{\nu}(-p)}
are

\displaystyle\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(p)\,R^{a}_{\nu}(-p)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}g^{2}v^{4}\langle A_{\mu}^{a}(p)\,A_{\nu}^{a}(-p)\rangle-\langle(\rho^{a}\partial_{\mu}h)(p)\,(\partial_{\nu}\rho^{a}h)(-p)\rangle+\frac{1}{4}p_{\mu}p_{\nu}\langle(\rho^{a}h)(p)\,(\rho^{a}h)(-p)\rangle

(5.65)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\langle(\rho^{a}\rho^{b})(p)\,(\rho^{a}\rho^{b})(-p)\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle(\rho^{a}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{b})(p)\,(\partial_{\nu}\rho^{a}\rho^{b})(-p)\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g^{2}v^{3}\langle A_{\mu}^{a}(p)\,(A_{\nu}^{a}h)(-p)\rangle-\frac{i}{4}gv^{3}p_{\nu}\langle A_{\mu}^{a}(p)\,\rho^{a}(-p)\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}v^{2}p_{\mu}p_{\nu}\langle\rho^{a}(x)\rho^{a}(y)\rangle+\frac{1}{6}g^{2}v^{2}\langle(\rho^{a}A_{\mu}^{b})(p)\,(\rho^{a}A_{\nu}^{b})(-p)\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{24}g^{2}v^{2}\langle(\rho^{a}\rho^{a}A_{\mu}^{b})(p)\,A^{b}(-p)\rangle+\frac{1}{8}g^{2}v^{2}\langle(h^{2}A_{\mu}^{a})(p)\,A_{\nu}^{a}(-p)\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g^{2}v^{2}\langle(hA_{\mu}^{a})(p)\,(hA_{\nu}^{a})(-p)\rangle+\frac{i}{4}v^{2}gp_{\mu}\langle(h\rho^{a})(p)\,A_{\nu}^{a}(-p)\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}gv^{2}\langle(\partial_{\mu}h\rho^{a})(p)\,A_{\nu}^{a}(-p)\rangle+\frac{i}{2}gv^{2}p_{\mu}\langle\rho^{a}(p)\,(hA_{\nu}^{a})(-p)\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}gv^{2}\varepsilon^{abc}\langle A^{a}(p)\,(\rho^{b}\partial_{\mu}\rho^{c})(-p)\rangle-\frac{igv^{2}}{2}\varepsilon^{abc}p_{\mu}\langle\rho^{a}(p)\,(\rho^{b}A_{\nu}^{c})(-p)\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}vp_{\mu}p_{\nu}\langle(h\rho^{a})(p)\,\rho^{a}(-p)\rangle-ivp_{\nu}\langle(\partial_{\mu}h\rho^{a})(p)\,\rho^{a}(-p)\rangle

where we have used the notation
\partial_{\mu}=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}
and
\partial_{\nu}=\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{\nu}}

2
2

2

The derivative here is not expressed in momentum space because for composite operators, the derivative will bring down different momenta depending on the configuration of the Feynman diagram.
.
The first term in expression (
5.65
) is the gauge field propagator
\braket{A_{\mu}^{a}(p)A_{\mu}^{a}(-p)}
, which means that
R^{a}_{\mu}
can be thought as a kind of BRST invariant extension of the elementary gauge field
A_{\mu}^{a}
. At tree-level, we find in fact

\displaystyle\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(x)\,R^{a}_{\nu}(y)}_{tree}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}g^{2}v^{4}\langle A_{\mu}^{a}(p),A_{\nu}^{a}(-p)\rangle+\frac{1}{4}v^{2}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\langle\rho^{a}(p),\rho^{a}(-p)\rangle

(5.66)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{16}g^{2}v^{4}\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)+\frac{3}{4}v^{2}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}(p),

where we can see that, apart from the constant factor
\frac{3}{4}v^{2}
appearing in the longitudinal sector, the transverse component reproduces exactly the transverse gauge tree-level propagator.

Since the correlation function
\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(p),R^{a}_{\nu}(-p)}
is independent from the gauge parameter
\xi
, due to the BRST invariant nature of the operator
R^{a}_{\mu}(x)
, we shall proceed as in the previous example by making use of
\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(p)\,R^{a}_{\nu}(-p)}_{\mathrm{u}nitary}=\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(p),R^{a}_{\nu}(-p)}
and evaluating the correlator at the one-loop order with the propagators given in (
5.47
), so that

\displaystyle\braket{R^{a}_{\mu}(p)\,R^{a}_{\nu}(-p)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}g^{2}v^{4}\langle A_{\mu}^{a}(p)\,A_{\nu}^{a}(-p)\rangle_{\mathrm{u}nitary}+\frac{1}{4}g^{2}v^{3}\langle A_{\mu}^{a}(p)\,(A_{\nu}^{a}h)(-p)\rangle_{\mathrm{u}nitary}

(5.67)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}g^{2}v^{2}\langle(h^{2}A_{\mu}^{a})(p)\,A_{\nu}^{a}(-p)\rangle_{\mathrm{u}nitary}+\frac{1}{4}g^{2}v^{2}\langle(hA_{\mu}^{a})(p)\,(hA_{\nu}^{a})(-p)\rangle_{\mathrm{u}nitary}

with the contributing diagrams shown in Figure
5.2
. Using dimensional regularization in the
\overline{MS}bar
-scheme with
(d=4-\epsilon)
and switching to momentum space, we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}g^{2}v^{4}\langle A_{\mu}^{a}(p)\,A_{\nu}^{a}(-p)\rangle_{\mathrm{u}nitary}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{g^{4}v^{4}}{32(4\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{-\frac{1}{\epsilon 6m^{4}m_{h}^{2}}\left(9m^{4}m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{2}\left(-9m^{6}-83m^{4}p^{2}-14m^{2}p^{4}+p^{6}\right)+54m^{8}\right)

(5.68)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{2p^{2}}\left(-m_{h}^{2}+m^{2}+7p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m^{2}p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\left(m^{4}m_{h}^{4}-m_{h}^{2}\left(m^{6}+47m^{4}p^{2}+16m^{2}p^{4}-2p^{6}\right)+54m^{6}p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2p^{2}}\left(-2m_{h}^{2}\left(m^{2}-p^{2}\right)+m_{h}^{4}+m^{4}-10m^{2}p^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+(1-x)m_{h}^{2}+m^{2}x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2m^{4}}\left(4m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\left(12m^{4}-20m^{2}p^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}+p^{2}(1-x)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6m^{4}p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\Big(3m^{4}m_{h}^{6}-3m_{h}^{4}\left(2m^{6}+9m^{4}p^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m_{h}^{2}\left(3m^{8}-9m^{6}p^{2}-2m^{4}p^{4}-26m^{2}p^{6}-2p^{8}\right)-54m^{8}p^{2}\Big)\Bigg\}\frac{\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{g^{4}v^{4}}{32(4\pi)^{2}m^{4}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\frac{3}{m_{h}^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{2}\left(3m^{2}+p^{2}\right)-3m_{h}^{4}-18m^{4}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{3m^{2}}{2p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{2}\left(p^{2}-m^{2}\right)+m_{h}^{4}-18m^{2}p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{3m_{h}^{2}}{2p^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}+5p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2p^{2}}\left(\left(m_{h}-m\right){}^{2}+p^{2}\right)\left(\left(m_{h}+m\right){}^{2}+p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+(1-x)m_{h}^{2}+m^{2}x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{4}\left(5p^{2}-2m^{2}\right)+m_{h}^{2}\left(m^{4}-m^{2}p^{2}\right)+m_{h}^{6}+6m^{4}p^{2}\right)\Bigg\}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}(p),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g^{2}v^{3}\braket{A^{a}_{\mu}(p)\,(A^{a}h)(-p)}_{\mathrm{u}nitary}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\frac{m^{2}}{m_{h}^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{2}\left(p^{2}-9m^{2}\right)+12m_{h}^{4}+54m^{4}\right)

(5.69)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}m_{h}^{2}}{2p^{2}}\left(-m_{h}^{2}+m^{2}+10p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{m^{4}}{2p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{2}\left(p^{2}-m^{2}\right)+m_{h}^{4}+54m^{2}p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}}{2p^{2}}\left(2p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}-5m^{2}\right)+\left(m^{2}-m_{h}^{2}\right){}^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+(1-x)m_{h}^{2}+xm^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}}{6p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\left(6m_{h}^{4}\left(m^{2}+6p^{2}\right)+m_{h}^{2}\left(-3m^{4}+9m^{2}p^{2}+2p^{4}\right)-3m_{h}^{6}+54m^{4}p^{2}\right)\Bigg\}\frac{\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)}{\left(m^{2}+p^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\frac{3}{m_{h}^{2}}\left(-m_{h}^{2}\left(3m^{2}+p^{2}\right)+m_{h}^{4}+18m^{4}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{3m^{2}}{2p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{2}\left(p^{2}-m^{2}\right)+m_{h}^{4}-18m^{2}p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{3m_{h}^{2}}{2p^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}+3p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{2}\left(\left(m-m_{h}\right){}^{2}+p^{2}\right)\left(\left(m_{h}+m\right){}^{2}+p^{2}\right)\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+(1-x)m_{h}^{2}+xm^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2p^{2}m_{h}^{2}}\left(\left(m_{h}^{3}-m^{2}m_{h}\right){}^{2}+p^{2}\left(-m^{2}m_{h}^{2}+3m_{h}^{4}+6m^{4}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\Bigg\}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}(p),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}g^{2}v^{2}\langle(h^{2}A_{\mu}^{a})(p)\,A_{\nu}^{a}(-p)\rangle_{\mathrm{u}nitary}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3m_{h}^{2}m^{2}}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\frac{2}{\epsilon}-\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+1\Bigg\}\Big(\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)

(5.70)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m^{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}(p)\Big),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}g^{2}v^{2}\langle(hA_{\mu}^{a})(p)\,(hA_{\nu}^{a})(-p)\rangle_{\mathrm{u}nitary}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon}(-3m_{h}^{2}+9m^{2}-p^{2})

(5.71)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{2p^{2}}\left(m^{2}+p^{2}-m_{h}^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+\frac{m^{2}}{2p^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2p^{2}}\left(2p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}-5m^{2}\right)+\left(m^{2}-m_{h}^{2}\right){}^{2}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+(1-x)m_{h}^{2}+xm^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6p^{2}}\left(3\left(m^{2}-m_{h}^{2}\right){}^{2}-9p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}+m^{2}\right)-2p^{4}\right)\Bigg\}\mathcal{P}_{\mu\nu}(p)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{3}{32\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(-m_{h}^{2}+3m^{2}+p^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}}{2p^{2}}\left(-m_{h}^{2}+m^{2}-p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{2p^{2}}\left(m_{h}^{2}-m^{2}+3p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2p^{2}}\left(\left(m-m_{h}\right){}^{2}+p^{2}\right)\left(\left(m_{h}+m\right){}^{2}+p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+(1-x)m_{h}^{2}+xm^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2p^{2}}\left(\left(m^{2}-m_{h}^{2}\right){}^{2}+3p^{2}m_{h}^{2}-p^{2}m^{2}\right)\Bigg\}\mathcal{L}_{\mu\nu}(p)\;.

Using the unity (
5.52
), we find that the transverse part of the propagator is given by

\displaystyle R(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}g^{2}v^{4}\Bigg(\frac{1}{p^{2}+m^{2}}+\frac{1}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\left(\Pi_{R}(p^{2})+\Pi_{\mathrm{d}iv}(p^{2})\right)\Bigg)+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2})

(5.72)

where the divergent part is, see also the comments in the Appendix
C.4
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathrm{d}iv}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{g^{2}}{\epsilon\pi^{2}}\Big(-\frac{h^{2}p^{4}}{32m^{4}}+\frac{9m^{4}}{16h^{2}}+\frac{9m^{2}p^{2}}{8h^{2}}+\frac{h^{2}p^{2}}{8m^{2}}+\frac{h^{2}}{16}-\frac{p^{6}}{48m^{4}}+\frac{23p^{4}}{96m^{2}}+\frac{7p^{2}}{8}\Big)\;,

(5.73)

while for the finite part we get

\displaystyle\Pi_{R}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{36\pi^{2}g^{2}v^{4}m_{h}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{6m^{4}\left(m_{h}^{4}+3m^{4}\right)-\frac{p^{4}m_{h}^{2}}{3}\left(9m_{h}^{2}+35m^{2}+4p^{2}\right)

(5.74)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle p^{2}\left(m^{4}m_{h}^{2}+10m^{2}m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{6}+36m^{6}\right)+m_{h}^{4}\left(-p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}+11m^{2}\right)-6m^{4}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m_{h}^{2}\left(2p^{4}\left(m_{h}^{2}-5m^{2}\right)+\left(m^{2}-m_{h}^{2}\right)^{2}p^{2}+p^{6}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+(1-x)m_{h}^{2}+xm^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m_{h}^{2}\left(48m^{6}-68m^{4}p^{2}-16m^{2}p^{4}+p^{6}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}\left(-48m^{4}-17m^{2}p^{2}+3p^{4}\right)+p^{2}m_{h}^{4}-54\left(m^{6}+2m^{4}p^{2}\right)\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}.

Since (
5.74
) contains terms of the order of
\frac{p^{4}}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\ln(p^{2})
and
\frac{p^{6}}{(p^{2}+m^{2})^{2}}\ln(p^{2})
,
we follow the steps (
3.51
)-(
3.53
) to find the resummed propagator in the one-loop approximation, namely

\displaystyle G_{R}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}g^{2}v^{4}\Big(\frac{1}{p^{2}+m_{h}^{2}-\hat{\Pi}_{R}(p^{2})}\Big)+C_{R}(p^{2})

(5.75)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\Pi}_{R}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{36\pi^{2}g^{2}v^{4}m_{h}^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{6m^{4}\left(m_{h}^{4}+3m^{4}\right)-\frac{p^{4}m_{h}^{2}}{3}\left(9m_{h}^{2}+35m^{2}+4p^{2}\right)

(5.76)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle p^{2}\left(m^{4}m_{h}^{2}+10m^{2}m_{h}^{4}+m_{h}^{6}+36m^{6}\right)+m_{h}^{4}\left(-p^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}+11m^{2}\right)-6m^{4}+p^{4}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m_{h}^{2}\left(m_{h}^{4}p^{2}-2m_{h}^{2}m^{4}-6m_{h}^{2}m^{2}p^{2}+12m^{6}+24m^{4}p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{x\left(m^{2}-p^{2}(x-1)\right)-(x-1)m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 33m_{h}^{2}\left(2m^{6}-m^{4}p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}-p^{2}(x-1)x}{\mu^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m^{2}\left(m_{h}^{2}\left(-48m^{4}-17m^{2}p^{2}+3p^{4}\right)+p^{2}m_{h}^{4}-54\left(m^{6}+2m^{4}p^{2}\right)\right)\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}

and

\displaystyle C_{R}(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{12(4\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{1}dx\Bigg\{\left(-18m^{2}+p^{2}\right)\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}(1-x)x+m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)

(5.77)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big((2\left(m_{h}^{2}-6m^{2}\right)+p^{2})\ln\left(\frac{p^{2}x(1-x)+(1-x)m_{h}^{2}+xm^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)\Bigg\}.

Looking now at the longitudinal part
L(p^{2})
, it turns out to be

\displaystyle L(p^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}v^{2}-\frac{1}{(4\pi)^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{m_{h}^{4}-3m_{h}^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m_{h}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)+9m^{4}-27m^{4}\ln\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)}{2m_{h}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(m_{h}^{2}-9\frac{m^{4}}{m_{h}^{2}}\right)\Bigg)\;.

(5.78)

As it happens in the tree-level case, expression (
5.78
) is independent from the momentum
p^{2}
, meaning that it does not correspond to the propagation of some physical mode, a feature which is expected to persist at higher orders.

5.4
Spectral properties

In this section, we will calculate the spectral properties associated with the correlation function obtained in the last section. For the employed techniques in obtaining the pole mass, residue and spectral density up to first order we refer to section
4.2.1
. In subsection
5.4.1
, we analyze the spectral properties of the elementary fields. In
5.4.3
, the spectral properties of the composite operators
O(x)
and
R^{a}_{\mu}(x)
are discussed.

Parameter values used in the spectral density functions.

We first discuss the spectral properties of the elementary fields: the scalar Higgs field
h(x)
and the transverse part of the gauge field
A^{a}_{\mu}(x)
. We will work with two sets of parameters, set out in Table
IV A
. All values are given in units of the energy scale
\mu
. Also, we have that
m^{2}=\frac{1}{4}g^{2}v^{2}
and
m_{h}^{2}=\lambda v^{2}
, so that
m^{2}=0.23\,\mu^{2}
and
m_{h}^{2}=0.192\,\mu^{2}
in Region I and
m^{2}=0.625\,\mu^{2}
and
m_{h}^{2}=0.205\,\mu^{2}
in Region II.

For the Higgs fields, following the steps from section
4.2.1
, we find the pole mass to first order in
\hbar
to be: for Region I

\displaystyle m_{h,\mathrm{p}ole}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.207\,\mu^{2},

(5.79)

and for Region II

\displaystyle m_{h,\mathrm{p}ole}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.206\,\mu^{2},

(5.80)

for all values of the parameter
\xi
. This means that while the Higgs propagator (
5.21
) is gauge dependent, the pole mass is gauge independent. This is in full agreement with the Nielsen identities of the
SU(2)
Higgs model studied in (
??
). The residue, however, is gauge dependent, as is depicted in Figure
5.3
.
For small values of
\xi
, including the Landau gauge
\xi=0
, the residue is not well-defined, and we cannot determine the spectral density function, as we will explain further in the next section.

Figure 5.3:
Dependence of the residue
Z
for the Higgs field propagator on the gauge parameter
\xi
, for Region I (Blue), and Region II (Orange).

In Figure
5.4
, we find the spectral density functions both regions, for three values of
\xi:1,2,5
. Looking at Region I, we see the first two-particle state appearing at
t=(m_{h}+m_{h})^{2}=0.768\,\mu^{2}
, followed by another two-particle state at
t=(m+m)^{2}=0.922\,\mu^{2}
. Then, we see that there is a negative contribution, different for each diagram, at
t=(\sqrt{\xi}m+\sqrt{\xi}m)^{2}
. This corresponds to the (unphysical) two-particle state of two Goldstone bosons. For
\xi<3
, this leads to a negative contribution for the spectral function, probably due to the large-momentum behaviour of the Higgs propagator (
5.21
), for a detailed discussion see Appendix
B.5
. For Region II, we find essentially the same behaviour: a Higgs two-particle state at
t=(m_{h}+m_{h})^{2}=0.81\,\mu^{2}
, and a gauge field two-particle state at
t=(m+m)^{2}=0.25\,\mu^{2}
. We also see a negative contribution different for each diagram at
t=(\sqrt{\xi}m+\sqrt{\xi}m)^{2}
, corresponding to the (unphysical) two-particle state of two Goldstone bosons.

.

For the gauge field propagator, following the steps from section

, we find the first-order pole mass of the transverse gauge field to be: for Region I

\displaystyle m_{\mathrm{p}ole}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.274\,\mu^{2}

(5.81)

and for Region II

\displaystyle m_{\mathrm{p}ole}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.065\,\mu^{2}

(5.82)

for all values of the parameter
\xi
, so that the pole mass is gauge independent. The residue is, however, gauge dependent as is depicted in Figure
5.5
. For small values of
\xi
the residue is not well-defined, as we will explain further in the next section.

Figure 5.5:
Dependence of the residue

for the gauge field propagator from the gauge parameter

, for Region I (Blue), and Region II (Orange).

In Figure

, we find the spectral density functions for both regions, for three values of
\xi
:
1,2,5
. Looking at Region I, we see the first two-particle state appearing at
t=(m_{h}+m)^{2}=0.843\,\mu^{2}
, followed by a two-particle state at
t=(m+m)^{2}=0.922\,\mu^{2}
. Then, we see that there is a negative contribution, different for each diagram, at
t=(m+\sqrt{\xi}m)^{2}
. This corresponds to the (unphysical) two-particle state of a gauge and Goldstone boson. For Region II, we find a gauge field two-particle state at
t=(m+m)^{2}=0.25\,\mu^{2}
. We also see a negative contribution different for each diagram at
t=(m+\sqrt{\xi}m)^{2}
, corresponding to the (unphysical) two-particle state of two Goldstone bosons.
